, we need to control the Zariski tangent space of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
at a general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n}
. As we will see below, although any
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n}
is a superabundant, irreducible uniruled locus in
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
whose dimension is independent of
n
, we will show that any such locus is contained, as a locally closed subset, in an irreducible component of the form
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(2-b)-mod}
. To do this, we start with:

Claim 3.29
.

For
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n}
general as above, the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
is such that
\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}}))=d-2\nu-2g+7
. In particular, we have

\operatorname{dim}(T_{[\mathcal{F}]}(B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)))=8g-2d-2\nu-11>\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n})=5g-8-d-\nu.

Proof of Claim
3.29
.

Recall that
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n}
general arises as an extension by
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+2\nu}(C)
general of
L=K_{C}-(A+M)
. In particular, since
h^{1}(N)=0
, then

H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\simeq H^{0}(N)\oplus H^{0}(K_{C}-A-M)\;{\rm and}\;H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})\simeq H^{0}(A+M).

Thus the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
reads also as

\begin{CD}\left[H^{0}(N)\oplus H^{0}(K_{C}-A-M)\right]&\;\otimes\;&\;H^{0}(A+M)&\;\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{\mathcal{F}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\;\;&H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}).\\
\end{CD}

By Claim
3.27
, we have
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=h^{1}(K_{C}-(A+M))=h^{0}(A+M)=3
. Consider the following diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&0&&0&&0&&\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[3.44444pt]
0&\rightarrow&N+A+M-K_{C}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}\otimes(A+M-K_{C})&\rightarrow&\mathcal{O}_{C}&\rightarrow&0\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[3.44444pt]
0&\rightarrow&N\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{(}K_{C}-A-M)\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}&\rightarrow&0\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[3.44444pt]
0&\rightarrow&\mathcal{O}_{C}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}\otimes N^{\vee}&\rightarrow&(K_{C}-A-M)\otimes N^{\vee}&\rightarrow&0\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[3.44444pt]
&&0&&0&&0&&\end{array}

which arises from the exact sequence defining
\mathcal{F}
tensored by its dual sequence. If we tensor the column in the middle by
\omega_{C}
, we get
H^{0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes(A+M))\hookrightarrow H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})
. From the isomorphism
H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})\simeq H^{0}(A+M)
, the Petri map of
\mathcal{F}
simply reads

\mu_{\mathcal{F}}:H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\otimes H^{0}(A+M)\to H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})
. By the previous injection

H^{0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes(A+M))\hookrightarrow H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})
we deduce that the kernel of
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
is the same as the kernel of the natural multiplication map
\mu^{\prime}:=\mu_{\mathcal{F},A+M}:H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\otimes H^{0}(A+M)\to H^{0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes(A+M))
.

Since
N
is general with
h^{1}(N)=0
, so it is
N+A+M
and thus
h^{1}(N+A+M)=0
. In particular, from the above diagram one has

H^{0}(N+A+M)\oplus H^{0}(\omega_{C})\simeq H^{0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes(A+M))\hookrightarrow H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}),

where the isomorphism follows from the first row whereas the inclusion comes from the middle column of the previous diagram.

Considering the natural multiplication map

\mu_{N,A+M}:H^{0}(N)\otimes H^{0}(A+M)\to H^{0}(N+A+M)
and the Petri map of
A+M
, namely
\mu_{A+M}:H^{0}(K_{C}-A-M)\otimes H^{0}(A+M)\to H^{0}(K_{C})
, from above we have that
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{N,A+M}\oplus\mu_{A+M}
and we can consider the following exact diagram:

\begin{array}[]{cclcccl}&0&&0&&0&\\[3.44444pt]
&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[3.44444pt]
0\to&\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{N,A+M})&\to&H^{0}(N)\otimes H^{0}(A+M)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{N,A+M}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(N+A+M)&\\[3.44444pt]
&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[3.44444pt]
0\to&\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}})&\to&[H^{0}(N)\oplus H^{0}(K_{C}-A-M)]\otimes H^{0}(A+M)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{\mathcal{F}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes(A+M))&\\[3.44444pt]
&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[3.44444pt]
0\to&\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{A+M})&\to&H^{0}(K_{C}-A-M)\otimes H^{0}(A+M)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{A+M}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(K_{C})&\\[3.44444pt]
&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[3.44444pt]
&0&&0&&0&\end{array}

To compute the kernel of the multiplication map
\mu_{N,A+M}
,
recall that
A+M
is globally generated (cf. Lemma
2.4
-
(b)
), therefore we have the exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{K}}\to H^{0}(A+M)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}\to A+M\to 0,

where
\mathcal{K}
denotes the
rank-
2
kernel vector bundle of the previous surjection. Tensoring by
\mathcal{F}
, one can complete to the following commutative diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&0&&0&&0&&\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[3.44444pt]
0&\rightarrow&(-A)\otimes N&\rightarrow&H^{0}(A)\otimes N&\rightarrow&A\otimes N&\rightarrow&0\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[3.44444pt]
0&\rightarrow&\mathcal{K}\otimes N&\rightarrow&H^{0}(A+M)\otimes N&\rightarrow&N\otimes(A+M)&\rightarrow&0\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[3.44444pt]
0&\rightarrow&N-M&\rightarrow&N&\rightarrow&N\otimes\mathcal{O}_{M}&\rightarrow&0\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[3.44444pt]
&&0&&0&&0&&.\end{array}

From
\mbox{deg}(N)\geq g-1+\nu
and from the generality of
N
, we have
h^{1}(N-A)=0
. Since
h^{0}(N-M)=0
, we get

\operatorname{dim}\left(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{N,A+M})\right)=h^{0}(N-A)=(d-2g+2+\nu+n-g+1)-\nu=d-3g+3+n.

On the other hand the Petri map
\mu_{A+M}
has a corank
2n+\nu-g-2
, since
\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}})=3g-6-2\delta-\nu=2n+\nu-g-2
, thus

\displaystyle\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{A+M}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3\,h^{0}(K_{C}-A-M)-g+(2n+\nu-g-2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3(g+2-\nu-n)-g+(2n+\nu-g-2)=g+4-n-2\nu.

∎

We want to show that the irreducible loci
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n}
, arising from bundles of type
(1-b)
in Lemma
3.2
and which actually exist under conditions as in Lemma
3.26
, are locally closed subschemes strictly contained (according to the chosen
d
) in irreducible components of the form
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,3,(2-b)-mod}
as in Proposition
3.19
, which instead have been constructed therein for any
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+3}{3}
and
2g-7+2\nu\leq d\leq 3g-4-\nu
.

Notice that when
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g-4}{3}
, as requested for the existence of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n}
in Lemma
3.26
, one clearly has
3g-7-\nu<3g-4-\nu
and
2g-7+2\nu<\frac{5g-10+\nu}{2}
; therefore, for any irreducible
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n}
there certainly exists an irreducible component of type
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(2-b)-mod}
as in Proposition
3.19
-
(ii)
. We moreover recall that

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(2-b)-mod})=8g-11-2\nu-2d>5g-8-d-\nu=\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n})

and that for

[\mathcal{F}_{w}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n}
general we have also

\operatorname{dim}(T_{[\mathcal{F}_{w}]}(B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)))=8g-2d-2\nu-11=\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(2-b)-mod}),

as proved in Claim
3.29
. Recall further that a general point

[\mathcal{F}_{u}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(2-b)-mod}
as in Proposition
3.19
-
(ii)
arises from an exact sequence of the form

(3.36)

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\to K_{C}-A\to 0,

where
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu}(C)
general,
h^{1}(K_{C}-A)=h^{0}(A)=2
and
u\in\mathcal{W}_{1}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,\,N)
general, namely the associated coboundary map
\partial_{u}
is such that
{\rm corank}(\partial_{u})=1
, whereas
[\mathcal{F}_{w}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n}
general as in Lemma
3.26
arises from a general extension

(3.37)

0\to N^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}_{w}\to K_{C}-(A+M)\to 0,

where
N^{\prime}\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu+n}(C)
general,
M
is an effective divisor of movable points of degree
\mbox{deg}(M)=n
on
C
such that
A+M\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\subset W^{2}_{\nu+n}(C)
is general in the component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}
as in Lemma
2.4
, i.e.
|A+M|=g^{2}_{\nu+n}
is base-point-free and birationally very ample, so
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{w})=h^{1}(K_{C}-(A+M))=h^{0}(A+M)=3
and the corresponding coboundary map
\partial_{w}
is surjective.

Proposition 3.30
.

Let
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g-4}{3}
,
\frac{5g-10+\nu}{2}\leq d\leq 3g-7-\nu
and
\frac{g+2-\nu}{2}\leq n\leq g-2\nu-1
be integers and let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
. Then any irreducible locus
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n}
as in Lemma
3.26
is strictly contained in a component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(2-b)-mod}
as in Proposition
3.19
, in such a way that
[\mathcal{F}_{w}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n}
general is a smooth point of the corresponding component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(2-b)-mod}
containing it.

In particular, the loci
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n}
arising from case
(1-b)
as in Lemma
3.2
can never fill-up an irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
.

Proof.

Let
[\mathcal{F}_{w}]\in\mathcal{B}_{3,n}
be a general point. By construction,
\mathcal{F}_{w}
fits into the extension (
3.37
), where
N^{\prime}\in\text{Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu+n}(C)
is general,
A+M\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}{2,1}}}\subset W^{2}_{\nu+n}(C)
is a general
g^{2}_{\nu+n}
, and
w\in\text{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A-M,N^{\prime})
is general. In particular, the coboundary map
\partial_{w}
is surjective. The surface scroll
F_{w}=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}_{w})
contains a special section
\Gamma
of degree
2g-2-\nu-n
and speciality
i(\Gamma)=3
, corresponding to the quotient
\mathcal{F}_{w}\twoheadrightarrow K_{C}-A-M
. This section is algebraically isolated (ai); indeed, its normal bundle
\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma/F_{w}}\simeq K_{C}-A-M-N^{\prime}
, the latter being general of degree
\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-A-M-N^{\prime})\leq\frac{g-2-3\nu}{2}<0
, hence
h^{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma/F_{w}})=0
. Composing the injection
N^{\prime}(-M)\hookrightarrow N^{\prime}
with (
3.37
) identifies
N^{\prime}(-M)
as a sub-line bundle, yielding:

(3.38)

0\to N^{\prime}(-M)\xrightarrow{\tilde{\sigma}}\mathcal{F}_{w}\to\mathcal{Q}\to 0.

The morphism
\tilde{\sigma}
induces a global section
\sigma
of
\mathcal{F}_{w}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(M)
. Tensoring (
3.37
) by
(N^{\prime})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(M)
gives:

0\to M\to\mathcal{F}_{w}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(M)\to K_{C}-A-N^{\prime}\to 0.

Since
\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-A-N^{\prime})\leq g-5
, the generality of
N^{\prime}
implies
h^{0}(K_{C}-A-N^{\prime})=0
. Consequently

(3.39)

h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{w}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(M))=h^{0}(M).

Claim 3.31
.

For
A+M\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{\nu+n}(C)
general in the component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}
as in Lemma
2.4
-
(b)
, one has
h^{0}(M)=1
.

Proof of Claim
3.31
.

Since
M
is effective, then
h^{0}(M)\geq 1
. Moreover
h^{0}(M)\leq 2
, because
h^{0}(A+M)=3
and

|A+M|
is base-point-free. We want to show that
h^{0}(M)=2
cannot occur.

If by contradiction we had
h^{0}(M)=2
, considering the natural multiplication map

\mu_{A,M}:H^{0}(A)\otimes H^{0}(M)\to H^{0}(A+M)

and the fact that
|A|
is base-point-free, by the base-point-free pencil trick we would get
{\rm ker}(\mu_{A,M})=h^{0}(M-A)\geq 1
because
h^{0}(A+M)=3
. Thus there would exist an effective divisor
D_{n-\nu}
of degree
\mbox{deg}(D_{n-\nu})=n-\nu
such that
M\sim A+D_{n-\nu}
so that

A+M\sim 2A+D_{n-\nu}
, contradicting that
A+M
is general in
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}
.
∎

From (
3.39
) and Claim
3.31
, we get
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{w}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(M))=1
. Similarly, tensoring (
3.37
) by
(N^{\prime})^{\vee}
we get

0\to\mathcal{O}_{C}\to\mathcal{F}_{w}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}\to K_{C}-A-M-N^{\prime}\to 0.

Since
K_{C}-A-M-N^{\prime}
is general, by generality of
N^{\prime}
, and since
\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-A-M-N^{\prime})=\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-A-N^{\prime})-n<\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-A-N^{\prime})
, reasoning as above we have
h^{0}(K_{C}-A-M-N^{\prime})=0
, so
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{w}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee})=h^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C})=1
. In other words, setting
\mathcal{E}:=\mathcal{F}_{w}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(M)
, from (
3.39
) and from above we have that

h^{0}(\mathcal{E})=h^{0}(\mathcal{E}(-M))=1.

Therefore, the unique global section
\sigma
of
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{F}_{w}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(M)
induced by (
3.38
) coincides with the unique global section of
\mathcal{E}(-M)=\mathcal{F}_{w}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}
which, from (
3.37
), does not vanish at any point of
C
as the cokernel of the injection
\mathcal{O}_{C}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}(-M)=\mathcal{F}_{w}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}
is locally free, being isomorphic to
K_{C}-A-M-N^{\prime}
. In other words,
\sigma
vanishes along
M
and we have

0\to\mathcal{O}_{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{F}_{w}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(M)\to(K_{C}-A-N^{\prime})\oplus\mathcal{O}_{M}\to 0.

Comparing with the definition of
\sigma
, we have
\mathcal{Q}\simeq(K_{C}-A-M)\oplus\mathcal{O}_{M}
and, replacing it into (
3.38
), we get that
\mathcal{F}_{w}
fits also in

(3.40)

0\to N^{\prime}(-M)\to\mathcal{F}_{w}\to(K_{C}-(A+M))\oplus\mathcal{O}_{M}\to 0,

i.e.
F_{w}
contains the reducible unisecant
\widetilde{\Gamma}:=\Gamma+f_{M}
as in (
1.4
) of degree
\mbox{deg}(\widetilde{\Gamma})=2g-2-\nu
and of speciality
i(\widetilde{\Gamma})=i(\Gamma)=3
as in (
1.9
), being
\Gamma
the section corresponding to the quotient line bundle
K_{C}-(A+M)
.

From (
3.40
),
\mathcal{F}_{w}
arises therefore also as an element
w
of the extension space

(3.41)

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}((K_{C}-(A+M))\oplus\mathcal{O}_{M},\;N^{\prime}(-M))\simeq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{M},\;N^{\prime}(-M))\oplus\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-(A+M),\;N^{\prime}(-M)).

We have
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-(A+M),\;N^{\prime}(-M))\simeq H^{1}(N^{\prime}+A-K_{C})
whereas, from
[
18
, III. Proposition 6.7, p. 235]
and from the fact that

\omega_{C}
and
N^{\prime}(-M)
are locally free, we have that

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{M},\;N^{\prime}(-M))\simeq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{M}\otimes\omega_{C},\;N^{\prime}(-M)\otimes\omega_{C})\simeq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{M}\otimes\omega_{C}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(M),\;\omega_{C});

the latter is such that

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{M}\otimes\omega_{C}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(M),\;\omega_{C})\simeq H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{M}\otimes\omega_{C}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(M))^{\vee}\simeq H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{M})^{\vee}\simeq H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{M}),

where the first isomorphism follows from
[
18
, III. Theorem 7.6, p. 243]
, the second and the third isomorphism from the fact that
\omega_{C}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(M)
is locally free whereas
\mathcal{O}_{M}
is a sky-scraper sheaf. Thus, replacing in (
3.41
), we get that

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}((K_{C}-(A+M))\oplus\mathcal{O}_{M},\;N^{\prime}(-M))\simeq H^{1}(N^{\prime}+A-K_{C})\oplus H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{M});

this means we have the following exact sequence

(3.42)

0\to H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{M})\to\mathrm{Ext}^{1}((K_{C}-(A+M))\oplus\mathcal{O}_{M},\;N^{\prime}(-M))\to H^{1}(N^{\prime}+A-K_{C})\to 0.

Similarly, since
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu}(C)
and
N^{\prime}\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu+n}(C)
are both general, we may consider
N=N^{\prime}-M
, therefore for
[\mathcal{F}_{u}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,\,(2-b)-mod}
general, we have

u\in\mathcal{W}_{1}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,\,N^{\prime}-M)\simeq H^{1}(N^{\prime}+A-K_{C}-M).

If we consider the exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{O}_{C}(-M)\to\mathcal{O}_{C}\to\mathcal{O}_{M}\to 0
and tensor it by

N^{\prime}+A-K_{C}
, we get

0\to N^{\prime}+A-K_{C}-M\to N^{\prime}+A-K_{C}\to\mathcal{O}_{M}\otimes(N^{\prime}+A-K_{C})\simeq\mathcal{O}_{M}\to 0.

Notice that, from the bounds on
d
and
n
,
\mbox{deg}(N^{\prime}+A-K_{C})=(d-2g+2+n+\nu)+\nu-(2g-2)=d-4g+4+n+2\nu\leq(3g-7-\nu)-4g+4+(g-2\nu-1)+2\nu=-4-\nu<0
, therefore
h^{0}(N^{\prime}+A-K_{C})=0
and the previous exact sequence gives in cohomology

(3.43)

0\to H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{M})\to H^{1}(N^{\prime}+A-K_{C}-M)\to H^{1}(N^{\prime}+A-K_{C})\to 0.

Comparing (
3.42
) and (
3.43
) we deduce the following geometric interpretation via
elementary transformations
of rank-
2
vector bundles, which will complete the proof: notice first that
H^{1}(N^{\prime}+A-K_{C})\simeq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N^{\prime})
and
that any
z\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N^{\prime})
gives rise to a rank-
2
vector bundle
\mathcal{F}_{z}
of degree
\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{F}_{z})=\mbox{deg}(N^{\prime})+\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-A)=d+n
(moreover, for
z\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N^{\prime})
general, one has also
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{z})=h^{1}(K_{C}-A)=2
). Let
F_{z}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}_{z})
; hence
F_{z}
contains a special section
\Gamma^{\prime}
of speciality
2
and of degree
\mbox{deg}(\Gamma^{\prime})=\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-A)=2g-2-\nu
, corresponding to the quotient line bundle
K_{C}-A
of
\mathcal{F}_{z}
. Moreover, since from above
h^{0}(K_{C}-N^{\prime}-A)=0
, then
\Gamma^{\prime}
is algebraically isolated (ai) (so fixed, cf. Definition
1.3
) on
F_{z}
.

Looking at (
3.43
), any
u\in H^{1}(N^{\prime}+A-K_{C}-M)\simeq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,\>N^{\prime}-M)
gives rise to a surface scroll
F_{u}=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}_{u})
obtained
by an elementary transformation of
F_{z}
as above, for
z\in H^{1}(N^{\prime}+A-K_{C})\simeq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,\,N^{\prime})
where such an elementary
transformation is performed by considering
n
points
q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\in F_{z}
, which sit on the (distinct) fibers
f_{M}
of
F_{z}
over some
M\in C^{(n)}
and which do not belong to the special section
\Gamma^{\prime}
; such an elementary transformation is obtained by first blowing-up
F_{z}
at each point of intersection between the fiber
f_{q_{i}}
and the
special section
\Gamma^{\prime}
and then by contracting the proper transform of the fiber
f_{q_{i}}
, for any
1\leq i\leq n
. This elementary transformation gives rise to a surface scroll
F_{u}
arising from
u\in H^{1}(N^{\prime}+A-K_{C}-M)\simeq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N^{\prime}-M)
; from the fact that
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{M})
is the kernel of the surjection (
3.43
), the same surface scroll
F_{z}
gives rise, by moving points
M
on
C
and by performing the elementary transformation as above, to positive dimensional families of surface scrolls
F_{u}
. Since for both
u
and
z
general the speciality of the corresponding bundles is
2
, by speciality reasons, it is clear that the same arguments apply for
z\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{1}}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,\,N^{\prime})
and
u\in\mathcal{W}_{1}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N^{\prime}-M)
general, which both give rise instead to bundles of speciality
3
.
By moving along the fibers
f_{M}
of
F_{z}
the points
q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}
of the elementary transformations described above and letting them specialize to the points of intersection between the fibers
f_{M}
with the fixed section
\Gamma^{\prime}
of
F_{z}
, performing the elementary transformation centered along the specialized points gives rise to a surface scroll
F_{w}
arising from a bundle
\mathcal{F}_{w}
fitting in (
3.37
), whose presentation is also equivalent to that in (
3.40
). This implies that
F_{u}

degenerates to
F_{w}
so also
\mathcal{F}_{u}
degenerates to
\mathcal{F}_{w}
, which shows that
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n}\subsetneq\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(2-b)-mod}
, according to the chosen degree
d
.

Finally, the fact that
[\mathcal{F}_{w}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,n}
general is a smooth point for
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(2-b)-mod}
follows from the computations in Claim
3.29
and the proof of the Proposition is complete.
∎

3.7.
Regular components from bundles of second type

Aside from the specific cases
d=2g-6+2\nu
in Proposition
3.13
-
(ii)
and
d=2g-7+2\nu
Proposition
3.19
-
(ii)
previously discussed, our constructions have yielded
superabundant
components. We now seek to construct
regular
components of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
, which necessitates restricting to the range

2g-2\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6,

as such components cannot exist for
d\geq\frac{10}{3}g-5
(cf. Remark
3.6
).

Since, from Propositions
3.7
and
3.30
, we know that bundles of type
(1\text{-}b)
and
(1\text{-}c)
cannot fill-up irreducible components and bundles of type
(1\text{-}a)
have been fully investigated in Proposition
3.13
in any possible degree
d
(and therein, the only case for a
regular
component whose general point arises as a bundle of type
(1-a)
is for
d=2g-6+2\nu
), we focus on bundles of
second type
or, if needed, on their natural
modifications
.

Since we want to produce components
\mathcal{B}
whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]
is a
second type
bundle of speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
, the most natural case to investigate is for stable bundles fitting into

(3.44)

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}\to 0,

where therefore
N
is forced to have speciality at least
2
. Specifically, we will prove the existence of a
regular
component
\mathcal{B}_{\text{reg},3}
where:

•

for
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-7
,
N\in\text{Pic}^{d-2g+4}(C)
is general;

•

for
3g-6\leq d\leq\frac{10g}{3}-6
,
N
is chosen in such a way that
\omega_{C}\otimes N^{\vee}
is a general
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
(cf. Lemma
2.4
).

\boxed{2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-7:\;\mbox{Existence of a regular component}}
As it will be clear from the proof of Proposition
3.32
below, to verify stability of bundles
\mathcal{F}
as in (
3.44
), we partition the range
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-7
into:

2g-2\leq d\leq\lfloor\tfrac{5g}{2}\rfloor-6\quad\text{and}\quad\lfloor\tfrac{5g}{2}\rfloor-5\leq d\leq 3g-7.

In the second range, the stability of a general
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\text{reg},3}
will follow from the stability of a specific bundle
\mathcal{F}_{w}
constructed in Claim
3.35
(Case 2). Since stability is an open condition in irreducible flat families, the existence of this special stable point will imply stability also for the general point of the component.

Proposition 3.32
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g\geqslant 7
, with
3\leqslant\nu<\frac{g}{2}
. For any integer
2g-2\leqslant d\leqslant 3g-7
,
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
contains a regular component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
which is uniruled and, for
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
general,
\mathcal{F}
fits into an exact sequence of the form

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}\to 0,

where

\bullet

N\in\operatorname{Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
is general (special, non-effective),

\bullet

\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{v}
, with
v
general in an irreducible component
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*}
of
\mathcal{W}_{2}\subsetneq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N)
as in (
2.2
), where
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*}
has expected codimension
c(2):=2d-10-4g
as in (
2.3
) and where
v\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*}
is such that
{\rm corank}(\partial_{v})=2
;

Furthermore
\mathcal{F}
also fits in an exact sequence of the form

0\to N(p)\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}(-p)\to 0,

where
p\in C
and
N(p)\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+3}(C)
are general. Moreover, if
\Gamma
(resp.
\Gamma_{p}
) denotes a section in the surface scroll
F:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F})
which corresponds to
\mathcal{F}\to\!\!\to\omega_{C}
(resp.
\mathcal{F}\to\!\!\to\omega_{C}(-p)
with
p\in C
general), then:

(i)

\Gamma
is not linearly isolated (not li) on
F
in the sense of Definition
1.3
, as it moves in a
2
-dimensional linear system, and
\omega_{C}
is not of minimal degree among possible special, effective quotients of
\mathcal{F}
.

(ii)

\Gamma_{p}
is neither algebraically isolated (not ai) in the sense of Definition
1.3
nor algebraically specially isolated (not asi) on
F
, i.e.

\operatorname{dim}_{\Gamma_{p}}\left(\mathcal{S}_{F}^{1,2g-3}\right)>0
, in the sense of (
1.10
). Moreover, the line bundle
\omega_{C}(-p)
is not of minimal degree among possible special quotients of
\mathcal{F}
.

Proof.

In the notation of (
1.17
), we set
L:=\omega_{C}
and let
N\in\text{Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
be a general line bundle. From the given bounds on
d
,
N
is non-effective with speciality
r:=h^{1}(N)=3g-3-d\geq 2
. We have
\ell:=h^{0}(\omega_{C})=g
and
m:=\operatorname{dim}\text{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N)=5g-5-d
. The conditions
\ell\geq r
and
m\geq\ell+1
are satisfied; thus, by Theorem
2.2
, for a general
u\in\text{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N)
,
\partial_{u}
is surjective. The degeneracy locus
\mathcal{W}_{1}\subsetneq\text{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N)
is irreducible and of expected codimension
c(1)=\ell-r+1
. Its general point corresponds to a bundle
\mathcal{F}_{z}
such that
\text{corank}(\partial_{z})=1
. By definition, we therefore have a strict inclusion
\mathcal{W}_{2}\subsetneq\mathcal{W}_{1}
since
\text{corank}(\partial_{v})\geq 2
for
v\in\mathcal{W}_{2}
by definition.

Note that
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is non-empty, as it contains the trivial extension
\mathcal{F}_{0}=N\oplus\omega_{C}
, whose coboundary map is the zero-map and
r\geq 4
in this range. The expected codimension of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is
c(2)=2(\ell-r+2)=2d+10-4g
. Our goal is to show the existence of an irreducible component
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{2}
of expected dimension
m-c(2)=9g-15-3d
, whose general point
v
satisfies
\text{corank}(\partial_{v})=2
. To establish this, we require the following preliminary result:

Claim 3.33
.

With assumptions as above, let
N\in\operatorname{Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
be general and let
\mathbb{G}:=\mathbb{G}(2,H^{0}(K_{C}-N))
be the Grassmannian parametrizing
2
-dimensional sub-vector spaces in
H^{0}(K_{C}-N)
, the latter of dimension
r=3g-3-d>2
. Let
\mu:=\mu_{K_{C}-N,\;K_{C}}:H^{0}(K_{C}-N)\otimes H^{0}(K_{C})\to H^{0}(2K_{C}-N)
be the natural multiplication map and, for any
[V_{2}]\in\mathbb{G}
, let
\mu_{V_{2}}
denote the restriction of the map
\mu
to the sub-vector space
V_{2}\otimes H^{0}(K_{C})\subset H^{0}(K_{C}-N)\otimes H^{0}(K_{C})
, namely
\mu_{V_{2}}:V_{2}\otimes H^{0}(K_{C})\to H^{0}(2K_{C}-N)
. Then, for
V_{2}\in\mathbb{G}
general, the map
\mu_{V_{2}}
is injective.

Proof of Claim
3.33
.

The proof is straightforward; in fact, a general
[V_{2}]\in\mathbb{G}
determines a
2
-dimensional sub-vector space of
H^{0}(K_{C}-N)
giving rise to a base-point-free linear pencil on
C
. Indeed, since
h^{0}(K_{C}-N)=3g-3-d\geqslant 4
, we can take
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in H^{0}(K_{C}-N)
general sections; if
p\in C
is such that
\sigma_{i}(p)=0
, for
i=1,2
, by generality of the sections, we would have
p\in{\rm Bs}(|K_{C}-N|)
so
h^{0}(N+p)=1
a contradiction because
N
is general of
\mbox{deg}(N)=d-2g+2\leq g-5
, from the bounds on
d
.

The injectivity of
\mu_{V_{2}}
follows at once from the base-point-free-pencil-trick since
\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{V_{2}})\cong H^{0}(N)
, which is zero, being
N
non-effective.
∎

Claim 3.34
.

With above assumptions, let
N\in\operatorname{Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
be general. Then, there exists a unique irreducible component
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*}
of
\mathcal{W}_{2}\subsetneq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N)
whose general
v\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*}
is such that
{\rm Coker}(\partial_{v})^{\vee}
corresponds to a general point in the Grassmannian
\mathbb{G}
. Moreover
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*}
is unirational, of the expected dimension as in (
2.3
), namely
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*})=9g-15-3d
.

Proof of Claim
3.34
.

Let
\mathbb{P}:=\mathbb{P}(\text{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N))\cong\mathbb{P}(H^{0}(2K_{C}-N)^{\vee})
. We define the incidence variety:

\mathfrak{I}:=\{(V_{2},\pi)\in\mathbb{G}\times\mathbb{P}\mid\text{im}(\mu_{V_{2}})\subseteq\pi\},

where
\mu_{V_{2}}:V_{2}\otimes H^{0}(K_{C})\to H^{0}(2K_{C}-N)
is the multiplication map as in Claim
3.33
. Given
m=5g-5-d
,
\ell=g
, and
r=3g-3-d
, we have
\ell\geq r>2
and
m\geq 2\ell+1
. Thus, by Claim
3.33
, for a general
[V_{2}]\in\mathbb{G}
, the map
\mu_{V_{2}}
is injective with
\operatorname{dim}(\text{im}(\mu_{V_{2}}))=2\ell\leq m-1
; in particular
\text{im}(\mu_{V_{2}})
is contained in at least one hyperplane
\pi
, ensuring that
\mathfrak{I}
is non-empty and that dominates
\mathbb{G}
.

Consider the natural projections
\mathbb{G}\xleftarrow{pr_{1}}\mathfrak{I}\xrightarrow{pr_{2}}\mathbb{P}
. For general
[V_{2}]\in\mathbb{G}
, the fiber
pr_{1}^{-1}([V_{2}])
is a linear system of hyperplanes of dimension
m-1-2\ell=3g-6-d>0
. Since
\mathbb{G}
is irreducible and all the
pr_{1}
-fibers are irreducible of the same dimension,
\mathfrak{I}
is irreducible with

\operatorname{dim}(\mathfrak{I})=\operatorname{dim}(\mathbb{G})+\operatorname{dim}(pr_{1}^{-1}(V_{2}))=(6g-10-2d)+(3g-6-d)=9g-16-3d.

Let
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}:=pr_{2}(\mathfrak{I})
; by construction,
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{W}_{2})
. For a general
[v]\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}
,
\text{corank}(\partial_{v})=2
with
\text{coker}(\partial_{v})^{\vee}\simeq V_{2}\in\mathbb{G}
. Note that
\operatorname{dim}(\mathfrak{I})
matches the expected dimension
m-1-c(2)
. Since
\mathfrak{I}
dominates
\mathbb{G}
and is irreducible,
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}
must be an irreducible component of
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{W}_{2})
. It must satisfy
\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})\geq\text{expdim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}_{2}})
, forcing the restriction
pr_{2}
to be generically finite so
\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})=9g-16-3d
.

Finally,
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}
is unirational (as a projective bundle over a rational Grassmannian), and its uniqueness follows from the irreducibility of
\mathfrak{I}
.∎

To construct
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
, we take into account a projective bundle
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d})\to S
over a suitable open dense subset
S\subseteq{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
, which parametrizes the family of
\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N))
’s as
N\in S
varies. Since from Claim
3.34
, for any such
N\in S
,
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}
is irreducible,
unirational
and of (constant) dimension
9g-16-3d
, one has an irreducible projective variety

(3.45)

(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}:=\left\{(N,[v])\in\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d})\;|\;[v]\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}\right\},

which is therefore
uniruled
over
S
, of dimension

(3.46)

\operatorname{dim}((\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot})=\operatorname{dim}(S)+\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})=g+(9g-16-3d)=10g-3d-16=\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}+2.

Our next aim is to construct a (rational) modular map
(\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*})^{Tot}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\dasharrow}}B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
. To define this map, we therefore need to show that the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{v}
arising from a general pair
(N,[v])\in(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
is stable. Using Theorem
1.11
, this will be done in the result below.

Claim 3.35
.

For
(N,[v])\in(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
general, the corresponding rank-
2
, degree
d
vector bundle
\mathcal{F}_{v}
is stable, of speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{v})=3
.

Proof of Claim
3.35
.

Since
\text{corank}(\partial_{v})=2
and
h^{1}(\omega_{C})=1
, it follows immediately that
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{v})=3
. We prove stability of the general bundle
\mathcal{F}_{v}
by considering two ranges for
d
:

(Case 1):
2g-2\leq d\leq\lfloor\frac{5g}{2}\rfloor-6
and   (Case 2):
\lfloor\frac{5g}{2}\rfloor-5\leq d\leq 3g-7
.

(Case 1): assume
2g-2\leq d\leq\lfloor\frac{5g}{2}\rfloor-6
and take
\delta=\mbox{deg}(\omega_{C})=2g-2
, so
2\delta-d=4g-4-d>2
, allowing us to apply Theorem
1.11
. Consider the morphism
\varphi:C\to\mathbb{P}
defined by the linear system
|2K_{C}-N|
, and let
X=\varphi(C)
. For an integer
\sigma\equiv 2\delta-d\pmod{2}
with
0\leq\sigma\leq 2\delta-d
, one has

\operatorname{dim}(\text{Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(2\delta-d+\sigma-2)}(X))=4g-7-d+\sigma.

This is strictly less than
\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})=9g-16-3d
provided
\sigma<5g-9-2d
. Given the upper bound on
d
,
5g-9-2d\geq 3
. Thus, we can choose
\sigma\in\{1,2\}
, which ensures that the general extension in
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}
is stable.

(Case 2): assume
\lfloor\frac{5g}{2}\rfloor-5\leq d\leq 3g-7
; in this range, we use the fact that stability is an open condition and we prove the existence of at least one peculiar stable bundle
\mathcal{F}_{w}
in the total family
(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
. Such a bundle will arise from the construction in Claim
3.36
below.

Recall that, by Lemma
2.3
, the component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subset W^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}(C)
is non-empty if
\rho(g,1,4g-4-d-t)\geq 0
, which is equivalent to
7g-10-2d-2t\geq 0
. This condition holds for
d\leq 3g-7
and appropriately chosen
t\leq\frac{g+2}{2}
. Based on this, we have the following:

Claim 3.36
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g\geqslant 7
, with

3\leqslant\nu<\frac{g}{2}
and let
d
be an integer with
\lfloor\frac{5g}{2}\rfloor-5\leqslant d\leqslant 3g-7
, and either
t:=\frac{g+2}{2}
, when both
g
and
d
are even, or
t:=\lceil\frac{g}{2}\rceil
otherwise. Assume that
D_{t}\in C^{(t)}
is general and that
N^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Pic}^{d-2g+2+t}(C)
is such that
\omega_{C}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}
is general in the component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subseteq W^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}(C)
as in Lemma
2.3
. Then there exists a stable, rank-
2
vector bundle
\mathcal{F}_{w}
of degree
d
and speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{w})=3
which fits into an exact sequence of the form

(3.47)

(w):\ \ \ 0\to N^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}_{w}\to\omega_{C}(-D_{t})\to 0,

Proof of Claim
3.36
.

Since
K_{C}-N^{\prime}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
is general,
h^{1}(N^{\prime})=2
and
h^{0}(N^{\prime})=d-3g+5+t\geq 0
. We define the degeneracy locus
\mathcal{W}_{2}\subset\text{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{t},N^{\prime})
as in (
2.2
).
Setting
\ell:=h^{0}(K_{C}-D_{t})=g-t
,
r:=2
, and
m:=5g-5-2t-d
, we have
\ell\geq r
and
m\geq\ell+1
for
d\leq 3g-7
and
t\leq\frac{g+2}{2}
. The locus
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is the dual of the cokernel of the multiplication map:

\Phi:H^{0}(K_{C}-D_{t})\otimes H^{0}(K_{C}-N^{\prime})\to H^{0}(2K_{C}-N^{\prime}-D_{t}).

Since
|K_{C}-N^{\prime}|
is a base-point-free pencil, the base-point-free pencil trick implies
\operatorname{ker}(\Phi)\simeq H^{0}(N^{\prime}-D_{t})=0
. Thus,
\Phi
is injective and:

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{2})=m-2\ell=3g-5-d\geq 2.

Any bundle
\mathcal{F}_{w}
corresponding to
w\in\mathcal{W}_{2}
has speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{w})=3
, as
\partial_{w}=0
and
h^{1}(N^{\prime})+h^{1}(K_{C}-D_{t})=2+1=3
.

Concerning stability, let
\delta=\mbox{deg}(\omega_{C}(-D_{t}))=2g-2-t
. Since
d\leq 4g-6-2t
, we have
2\delta-d\geq 2
. We apply Theorem
1.11
using the morphism
\varphi:C\to\mathbb{P}(H^{0}(2K_{C}-N^{\prime}-D_{t})^{\vee})
and let
X=\varphi(C)
. For
\sigma\equiv 2\delta-d\pmod{2}
, the condition for stability
\mathcal{W}_{2}\not\subseteq\text{Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(2\delta-d+\sigma-2)}(X)
reduces to
\operatorname{dim}(\text{Sec})\leq\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{2})
, which is satisfied if
\sigma\leq-g+1+2t
. Based on the parity of
g
and
d
:

\bullet
If
g
is odd (
t=\frac{g+1}{2}
) or
g
and
d
are even (
2t=g+2
), we take
\sigma\in\{1,2\}
. The secant variety is either of lower dimension than
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{W}_{2})
or it is a non-linear variety of the same dimension; in either case, the general
w\in\mathcal{W}_{2}
yields a stable bundle
\mathcal{F}_{w}
.

\bullet
If otherwise
g
is even and
d
is odd (
t=\frac{g}{2}
), we take
\sigma=1
. Again,
\text{Sec}(X)
is a non-linear variety of the same dimension as the linear space
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{W}_{2})
, ensuring the general bundle is stable.

This concludes the proof of the stability of the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{w}
.
∎

We can therefore go on with the proof of (Case 2) of Claim
3.35
. A further key step will be the following:

Claim 3.37
.

For
D_{t}\in C^{(t)}
,
\omega_{C}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subseteq W^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}(C)
and

w\in\mathcal{W}_{2}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{t},N^{\prime})
general elements as in (the statement and the proof of) Claim
3.36
, the pair
(N^{\prime}-D_{t},[w])
belongs to the closure of
(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
as in (
3.45
).

Proof of Claim
3.37
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{w}
be the bundle constructed in Claim
3.36
with
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{w})=3
and
\partial_{w}=0
. Since
N^{\prime}=K_{C}-g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}
, Lemma
3.4
-(ii) implies that
\mathcal{F}_{w}
admits a second presentation:

(3.48)

0\to N^{\prime}-D_{t}\to\mathcal{F}_{w}\to\omega_{C}\to 0.

The corresponding coboundary map
\widetilde{\partial_{w}}
satisfies
\text{corank}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})=2
. Note that
\mbox{deg}(N^{\prime}-D_{t})=d-2g+2
, matching the degree of a general
N
in Claim
3.34
. Since
d\leq 3g-7
, we have
h^{0}(N^{\prime}-D_{t})=0
and
h^{1}(N^{\prime}-D_{t})=3g-3-d
, ensuring that
N^{\prime}-D_{t}
has the same cohomology as a general
N
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}:=\mathbb{G}(2,H^{0}(K_{C}-(N^{\prime}-D_{t})))
. Presentation (
3.48
) gives a point
[\text{coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}]\in\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}
. To determine the properties of such a point, we analyze the dual sequences:

\text{coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}\oplus H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C})\simeq H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{w}^{\vee})\simeq H^{0}(g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t})\oplus H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C}(D_{t})).

Let
\{1,f\}
be a basis for
H^{0}(g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t})
and
h\in H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C}(D_{t}))
be a non-zero global section. Then
\text{coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}
is generated by
\{f,h\}
. By the base-point-free pencil trick, the restricted multiplication map
\mu_{\text{coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}}
is injective since

\operatorname{dim}({\rm ker}(\mu_{{\rm coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}}))\leq h^{0}(K_{C}-g^{1}-(D_{t}-G\cap D_{t}))=0
. By semicontinuity, the injectivity of the multiplication map holds for a general
V_{2}\in\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}
. Taking into account what proved above, namely that:

\bullet

N^{\prime}-D_{t}=K_{C}-g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}-D_{t}
has the same degree and cohomology of
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
general as in Claims
3.33
,
3.34
and that the same occurs for
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N^{\prime}-D_{t})\simeq H^{1}(N^{\prime}-D_{t}-K_{C})\simeq H^{0}(2K_{C}-N^{\prime}+D_{t})^{\vee}=H^{0}(K_{C}+D_{t}+g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t})^{\vee}
and
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N)\simeq H^{1}(N-K_{C})=H^{0}(2K_{C}-N)^{\vee}
,

\bullet

[V_{2}]\in\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}
general and
[{\rm coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}]\in\Sigma\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}
both define sub-linear series of
|K_{C}-N^{\prime}+D_{t}|=|g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}+D_{t}|
for which both maps

\mu_{V_{2}}
and
\mu_{{\rm coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}}
are injective,

then one can reproduce verbatim the same proof as in Claim
3.34
and deduces that the irreducible locus
\Sigma\subseteq\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}
filled-up by points of the form
[{\rm coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}]
is contained in the image, via the map
pr_{1}
, of the relevant incidence variety
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}
dominating
\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}
, as in the proof therein. This implies that
w
belongs to the unique irreducible component
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*}}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N^{\prime}-D_{t})
arising as the image via the generically finite map
pr_{2}
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}
. Therefore when
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
general specializes to
N^{\prime}-D_{t}=K_{C}-g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}-D_{t}\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
, by uniqueness reasons the component
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N)
specializes to the component
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*}}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N^{\prime}-D_{t})
which proves that the pair
(N^{\prime}-D_{t},[w])
belongs to the closure of
(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
as in (
3.45
), so the proof of Claim
3.37
is completed.
∎

In summary, Claim
3.37
establishes that the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{w}
as in Claim
3.36
represents a peculiar point within the family
(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
. Given its stability, as proved in Claim
3.36
, and the fact that stability is an open condition it follows that, for general
v\in(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
, also the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{v}
is stable. This completes the proof of Claim
3.35
.
∎

Based on such stability results, we may define a rational modular map:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\dashrightarrow}}&U_{C}(d)\\
(N,[v])&\longrightarrow&[\mathcal{F}_{v}],\end{array}

where the image
\text{im}(\pi)
is contained within
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
. Our subsequent analysis will prove that the general fiber of
\pi
has dimension
2
, which is related to the dimension of the linear system of canonical sections in the surface scroll

F=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F})
, for
[\mathcal{F}]\in\text{im}(\pi)
general, as in Proposition
3.32
-
(i)
. Consequently, we will have
\operatorname{dim}(\text{im}(\pi))=\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}
so the closure of this image in
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
is the candidate to yield the regular component
\mathcal{B}_{\text{reg},3}
.

Claim 3.38
.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
be the closure of the locus
{\rm im}(\pi)
as above in
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
. Then
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
is uniruled, of dimension

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3})=\rho^{k_{3}}_{d}=10g-18-3d
.

Proof of Claim
3.38
.

The uniruledness of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
follows from the fact that its dense subset
{\rm im}(\pi)
is dominated by
(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
, which is uniruled over
S
.

Since
\operatorname{dim}((\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot})=\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}+2
, to prove
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3})=\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}
it suffices to show
\operatorname{dim}(\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{v}]))=2
for general
[\mathcal{F}_{v}]\in{\rm im}(\pi)
. Let
[\mathcal{F}_{v}]
be such a general point, so arising from
[v]\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}\subset\mathbb{P}(\text{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N))
with
N
general. Since
\mathcal{F}_{v^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{v}
implies
\det(\mathcal{F}_{v^{\prime}})\simeq\det(\mathcal{F}_{v})
, we must have
N^{\prime}\simeq N
, hence the fiber
\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{v}])
lies within the same
\mathbb{P}(\text{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N))
. Any isomorphism
\varphi:\mathcal{F}_{v^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{F}_{v}
induces the diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccl}0\to&N&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{F}_{v^{\prime}}&\to&\omega_{C}&\to 0\\
&&&\downarrow^{\varphi}&&&\\
0\to&N&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{F}_{v}&\to&\omega_{C}&\to 0.\end{array}

If
[v]=[v^{\prime}]
, then
\varphi\in\mathbb{C}^{*}
(by stability of
\mathcal{F}_{v}
) so the maps
\varphi\circ\iota_{1}
and
\iota_{2}
determine sections in
H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{v}\otimes N^{\vee})
. Since
\det(\mathcal{F}_{v})=\omega_{C}\otimes N
and
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{v})=3
, Serre duality yields
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{v}\otimes N^{\vee})=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{v})=3
. Geometrically, the fiber
\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{v}])
contains the linear system
|\Gamma|\simeq\mathbb{P}^{2}
, where
\Gamma\subset\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}_{v})
is the canonical section corresponding to the quotient
\mathcal{F}_{v}\to\omega_{C}
. If otherwise
[v]\neq[v^{\prime}]
, we would find linearly independent global sections in
H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{v}\otimes N^{\vee})
that do not arise from a homothety, implying
\operatorname{dim}(|\mathcal{O}_{F_{u}}(\Gamma)|)>2
, a contradiction. Thus
\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{v}])\simeq\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and
\operatorname{dim}(\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{v}]))=2
.
∎

Claim 3.39
.

For
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
general as above, the corresponding bundle
\mathcal{F}
admits as quotient line bundles also
\omega_{C}(-p)
, for
p\in C
general. If
\Gamma
(resp.,
\Gamma_{p}
) denote any section
F:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F})
corresponding to the quotient line bundle of
\mathcal{F}
given by
\omega_{C}
(resp.,
\omega_{C}(-p)
, with
p\in C
general), then:

(i)

\Gamma
is not linearly isolated (not li) on
F
in the sense of Definition
1.3
, and
\omega_{C}
is not of minimal degree among possible special quotients of
\mathcal{F}
.

(ii)

\Gamma_{p}
is neither algebraically isolated (not ai) in the sense of Definition
1.3
nor algebraically specially isolated (not asi) on
F
, i.e.

\operatorname{dim}_{\Gamma_{p}}\left(\mathcal{S}_{F}^{1,2g-3}\right)>0
, in the sense of (
1.10
). Moreover, the line bundle
\omega_{C}(-p)
is not of minimal degree among possible special quotients of
\mathcal{F}
.

Proof of Claim
3.39
.

For
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\text{reg},3}
general, we already know that the canonical linear system
|\Gamma|
on
F
satisfies
\operatorname{dim}(|\Gamma|)=2
. Consequently,
\Gamma
is clearly not linearly isolated (not li), according to Definition
1.3
. By arguments analogous to the proof of Claim
3.20
, the bundle
\mathcal{F}
admits
\omega_{C}(-p)
as a quotient for a general
p\in C
. This implies that
\omega_{C}
is not of minimal degree among the special quotients of
\mathcal{F}
, proving part
(i)
.

Determinantal considerations show that
\mathcal{F}
also fits into the extension:

0\to N(p)\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}(-p)\to 0,

where
N(p)\in\text{Pic}^{d-2g+3}(C)
is general. The normal bundle of the corresponding section
\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma_{p}/F}\simeq K_{C}-2p-N
is thus general, of degree
4g-6-d\geq g+1
. It then follows that
\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma_{p}/F}
is non-special and effective with
h^{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma_{p}/F})\geq 2
. Thus,
[\Gamma_{p}]
is a smooth point of the Hilbert scheme
\text{Div}_{F}^{1,2g-3}
, with
\operatorname{dim}_{[\Gamma_{p}]}(\text{Div}_{F}^{1,2g-3})\geq 2
. In this context,
\Gamma_{p}
is not algebraically isolated (not ai).

Since these properties hold for a general
p\in C
, there exists an open dense subset
U\subseteq C
that maps non-constantly to
\text{Div}_{F}^{1,2g-3}
, parameterizing the algebraic family of sections
\{\Gamma_{p}\}_{p\in U}
. This proves that
\Gamma_{p}
, as a section corresponding to
\omega_{C}(-p)
, is not algebraically specially isolated (not asi); specifically
\operatorname{dim}_{\Gamma_{p}}\left(\mathcal{S}_{F}^{1,\delta}\right)>0
as in (
1.10
). Given
h^{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma_{p}/F})\geq 2
, the proof of
(ii)
is completed with the use of Proposition
1.4
-(2-ii).
∎

To conclude the proof of Proposition
3.32
, we are left with showing the following result.

Claim 3.40
.

For
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
general as above, the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
is injective. In particular,
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
is an irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
which is regular and uniruled.

Proof of Claim
3.40
.

The uniruledness and the dimension of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
were established in Claim
3.38
. To prove regularity, we need to show the injectivity of the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
for a general
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
.

Such a bundle fits into the extension:

(3.49)

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}\to 0,

where
N\in\text{Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
is general and
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
. Let

\mathcal{E}_{v}:=\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}
and
g^{r}_{g+r}:=K_{C}-N
. Since
K_{C}-N
is general of degree at least
g+3
, it is non-special and base-point-free. The dual of (
3.49
) yields:

(3.50)

0\to\mathcal{O}_{C}\to\mathcal{E}_{v}\to g^{r}_{g+r}\to 0,

with
h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v})=3
. Since
\mathcal{F}
arises from
(N,[v])\in(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
general, one has that

0\to H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C})\to H^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v})\to V_{2}\to 0\ \ \ \mbox{ with }h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v})=3.

for a general
[V_{2}]\in\mathbb{G}:=\mathbb{G}(2,H^{0}(K_{C}-N))=\mathbb{G}(2,H^{0}(g^{r}_{g+r}))
. Considering that a non-special line bundle is base-point-free, one has that
K_{C}-N
is base-point-free and so it is
V_{2}
general as above.

This fact allows us to show that
\mathcal{E}_{v}
is globally generated. Indeed, considering the evaluation at an arbitrary
p\in C
gives

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&0&&0&&0&&\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[3.44444pt]
0&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C}(-p))&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v}(-p))&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\longrightarrow}}&{\rm im}(\alpha)&\rightarrow&0\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[3.44444pt]
0&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C})&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v})&\longrightarrow&V_{2}&\rightarrow&0,\end{array}

where
{\rm im}(\alpha)\subset H^{0}(g^{r}_{g+r}(-p))
.
One notices that
{\rm im}(\alpha)
is a subspace of
V_{2}
and so
\operatorname{dim}({\rm im}(\alpha))\leq 2
. On the one hand,
V_{2}
gives rise to a base-point-free linear pencil of
g^{r}_{g+r}
, whereas
{\rm im}(\alpha)
gives rise to a sub-series of
g^{r}_{g+r}(-p)\subset g^{r}_{g+r}
. This implies that
{\rm im}(\alpha)
is a proper subspace of
V_{2}
, whence
\operatorname{dim}({\rm im}(\alpha))\leq 1
. Therefore one gets
h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v}(-p))\leq 1
, equivalently
h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v}(-p))\leq h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v})-2
so the bundle
\mathcal{E}_{v}=\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{v}^{\vee}
is globally generated. The evaluation sequence for
\mathcal{E}_{v}
is

0\to\det(\mathcal{E}_{v})^{\vee}=-g^{r}_{g+r}\to\mathcal{O}_{C}\otimes H^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v})\to\mathcal{E}_{v}\to 0;

tensoring it by
\mathcal{F}
and taking cohomology gives the
Petri sequence
:

(3.51)

0\to H^{0}(\mathcal{F}(-g^{r}_{g+r}))\to H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\otimes H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\mu_{\mathcal{F}}}H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}).

Thus,
\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}})\simeq H^{0}(\mathcal{F}(-g^{r}_{g+r}))
. From (
3.49
), we have the sequence

0\to N-g^{r}_{g+r}\to\mathcal{F}(-g^{r}_{g+r})\to K_{C}-g^{r}_{g+r}\to 0.

By the generality of
N
and the definition of
g^{r}_{g+r}
, we have
h^{0}(N)=0
and
h^{0}(K_{C}-g^{r}_{g+r})=h^{0}(N)=0
. This implies
H^{0}(\mathcal{F}(-g^{r}_{g+r}))=0
, proving that
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
is injective and so the claim.
∎

The previous arguments conclude the proof of Proposition
3.32
.
∎

\boxed{3g-6\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6:\;\mbox{Existence of a regular component}}
We now address the remaining cases in which the existence of a
regular
component is still possible, focusing on the range
3g-6\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
. Similarly as in Proposition
3.19
, in order to ease constructions, we slightly modify the presentations of
second type
bundles. Specifically, we will consider kernel line bundles
N
arising from a suitable refinement of case
(2\text{-}c)
in Proposition
3.12
. Before proceeding with the construction of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
, we state the following preliminary result.

Lemma 3.41
.

Let
g\geq 9
,
4\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
and
t\leq g+2
be positive integers and let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
. Let
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
be general in such a component as in Lemma
2.4
, i.e.
h^{0}(g^{2}_{t})=3
and
\frac{2}{3}g+2\leq t\leq g+2
by
\rho(g,2,t)=3t-2g-6\geq 0
. Consider
\mathcal{W}_{2}\subsetneq{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
as in (
2.2
) and assume there exists an irreducible, positive dimensional locus
\mathcal{U}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{2}
, whose general point
u\in\mathcal{U}_{2}
is such that
{\rm corank}(\partial_{u})=2
. Let
\mathcal{F}_{u}
be the rank-
2
vector bundle arising from such a general
u\in\mathcal{U}_{2}
, so fitting in an exact sequence of the form:

(3.52)

(u)\ \ \ 0\to K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\to K_{C}\to 0.

Let
F_{u}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}_{u})
denote the surface scroll associated to
\mathcal{F}_{u}
. Then:

(i)
the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
fits also in an exact sequence of the form:

(u_{p})\ \ \ \ \ 0\to K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\to K_{C}-p\to 0,

where
p\in C
is general;

(ii)
for
p\in C
general, the section
\Gamma_{p}
corresponding to
\mathcal{F}_{u}\to\!\!\to K_{C}-p
is linearly isolated in
F_{u}
, in the sense of Definition
1.3
;

(iii)
for
p\in C
general, the sections
\Gamma_{p}
are all algebraically equivalent on
F_{u}
, belonging to a
1
-dimensional, integral component of
{\rm Div}^{1,2g-3}_{F_{u}}
;

(iv)
for
p\in C
general,
K_{C}-p
is of minimal degree among effective and special quotients of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
;

(v)

\mathcal{F}_{u}
is stable for either
d\geq 3g-6
, when
\frac{g+3}{3}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
, or
d\geq 4g-3-4\nu
, when
4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
.

Proof.

(i)
The proof is similar to that in Proposition
3.5
; since
\mathcal{F}_{u}
fits in (
3.52
) and is of speciality
3
, the coboundary map
\partial_{u}
has corank
2
. Since
\det(\mathcal{F}_{u})\simeq 2K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}
, duality implies
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}-(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}))=3
. Setting
F_{u}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}_{u})
, the canonical section
\Gamma
of
F_{u}
satisfies
\operatorname{dim}(|\Gamma|)=2
by (
1.8
). Thus,
F_{u}
contains a section
\Gamma_{p}
corresponding to
\omega_{C}(-p)
, and
\mathcal{F}_{u}
fits in extension
(u_{p})
.

(ii)
For
q\in F_{u}
general point with projection
p\in C
, the linear pencil
|\mathcal{I}_{q/F_{u}}(\Gamma)|
contains a unique section
\Gamma_{p}
after splitting the fiber
f_{p}
. Thus
\Gamma_{p}
is linearly isolated, meaning
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-K_{C}-p+g^{2}_{t}))=1
, which is equivalent to
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(p))=1
.

(iii)
By (
1.6
),
\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma_{p}/F_{u}}\simeq g^{2}_{t}-2p
. Since general
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
is birationally very ample by Lemma
2.4
, one has
h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-2p)=1
, so
\operatorname{dim}_{[\Gamma_{p}]}(\text{Div}^{1,2g-3}_{F_{u}})=1
. The locus
\mathfrak{G}_{U}:=\{\Gamma_{p}\}_{p\in U}
is a 1-dimensional, generically smooth component of the Hilbert scheme of unisecants of
F_{u}
of degree
\delta=2g-3
so sections
\Gamma_{p}
are algebraically (but not linearly) equivalent and all of speciality
1
.

(iv)
If
\omega_{C}(-p)
were not of minimal degree among effective and special quotients of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
, then there would exist
q\in C
for which
\mathcal{F}_{u}
would also fit in

0\to K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p+q\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\to K_{C}-p-q\to 0.

This would imply
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\leq h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p-q)+h^{0}(p+q)=h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p-q)+1
, as
C
is not hyperelliptic. Since a general
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
is birationally very ample by Lemma
2.4
, we haave
h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p-q)=1
, for
p\in C
general, hence, from above,
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\leq 2
. This would contradict
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=3
.

(v)
Concerning stability, assume first by contradiction that
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is unstable with maximal subbundle
M
,
\mbox{deg}(M)>2g-2-\frac{t}{2}
. Since from
(i)

\mathcal{F}_{u}
fits also in
(u_{p})
, for
p\in C
general, by determinantal reasons one has the following diagram:

(3.53)

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&&&0&&&&\\[3.44444pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[3.44444pt]
&&&&M&&&&\\[3.44444pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&\\[3.44444pt]
0&\rightarrow&K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}&\rightarrow&K_{C}-p&\rightarrow&0\\[3.44444pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[3.44444pt]
&&&&2K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}-M&&&&\\[3.44444pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[3.44444pt]
&&&&0&&&&\end{array}

Since
\mbox{deg}(M)>\frac{d}{2}=2g-2-\frac{t}{2}
whereas

\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p)=2g-1-t
and since
t>2
, one has
\mbox{deg}(M)>\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p)
. Hence there is no line-bundle map from
M
to
K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p
, so the composition of the two maps
M\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{u}
and
\mathcal{F}_{u}\to\!\!\!\to K_{C}-p
from (
3.53
) gives a non-zero line-bundle map
M\to K_{C}-p
which, necessarily, has to be injective. Therefore
M=K_{C}-p-E_{e,p}
for some effective divisor
E_{e,p}
of degree
e>0
, otherwise
M
would be isomorphic to
K_{C}-p
and, in such a case,
\mathcal{F}_{u}
would correspond to the zero-vector
\mathcal{F}_{0}
of the extension space, contradicting that
\mathcal{F}_{u}
arises from a general element of a positive dimensional irreducible locus
\mathcal{U}_{2}
. For determinantal reasons, we have therefore:

(3.54)

0\to M=K_{C}-p-E_{e,p}\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\to K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p+E_{e,p}\to 0,

which yields
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\leq h^{0}(p+E_{e,p})+h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p-E_{e,p})
. On the one hand, a general
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
is birationally very ample by Lemma
2.4
, so
h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p-E_{e,p})\leq 1
, being
p\in C
general and
E_{e,p}
effective. From (
3.54
) one gets
h^{0}(p+E_{e,p})\geq 2
since
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=3
. In other words,
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in W^{1}_{e+1}(C)
.

We remark that
e+1:=\mbox{deg}(p+E_{e,p})<\frac{t}{2}
, due to
\mbox{deg}(M)>\frac{d}{2}=2g-2-\frac{t}{2}
. Moreover, from the assumptions,
t\leq g+2
so
\frac{t}{2}\leq\frac{g+2}{2}
. Since
e+1<\frac{t}{2}\leq\frac{g+2}{2}
, from Lemma
2.3
it follows that
W^{1}_{e+1}(C)=\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}
, namely
|p+E_{e,p}|=g^{1}_{\nu}+B_{e+1-\nu}
, where
B_{e+1-\nu}
denotes an effective divisor of base points of
|p+E_{e,p}|
. This means that
h^{0}(p+E_{e,p}-g^{1}_{\nu})\geq 1
. From Lemma
2.4
, if we consider the maximal splitting vector associated to
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
general, it is
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\vec{w}_{2,0})=B(\nu-3,t+1-g-\nu)\oplus B(3,0)
. Since
g^{1}_{\nu}
corresponds to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)
, one sees that

h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-g^{1}_{\nu})=h^{0}(B(\nu-3,t-g-\nu)\oplus B(3,-1))=0

so, a fortiori,

h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-(p+E_{e,p}))=0
, since
|p+E_{e,p}|=g^{1}_{\nu}+B_{e+1-\nu}
. Using this, combined with
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=3
in the previous exact sequence, we get that
h^{0}(p+E_{e,p})\geq 3
, i.e.
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in W^{2}_{e+1}(C)
.

Since
\mbox{deg}(p+E_{e,p})<\frac{g+2}{2}
and since
\nu\geq 4
from the assumptions, by classification in Lemma
2.4
we have that

W^{2}_{e+1}(C)=\emptyset
when
\frac{g+3}{3}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
(this is because
e+1<\frac{g+2}{2}
and the latter integer is strictly less than all the upper-bounds appearing in the first line of the lists of cases occurring in
(iii)
,
(iv)
,
(v)
,
(vi)
and
(vii)
of Lemma
2.4
), this gives a contradiction when

d\geq 3g-6\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\;\frac{g+3}{3}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2},

as, from
(u)
, one has
d=4g-4-t
with
t\leq g+2
.

When otherwise
4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
, from Lemma
2.4
,
W^{2}_{e+1}(C)
is not empty and irreducible consisting only of
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}
. In particular, one must have

e+1-2\nu\geq 0
, so one has
2\nu\leq e+1<\frac{t}{2}
, which forces
t>4\nu
and so

d=4g-4-t<4g-4-4\nu
. Therefore, we get contradiction for
d\geq 4g-4-4\nu\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\;4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
.

Assume now by contradiction that
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is strictly semi-stable, which may occur only when
d
is even. This means that there exists a maximal sub-line bundle
M\subset\mathcal{F}_{u}
, with
\mbox{deg}(M)=\frac{d}{2}=2g-2-\frac{t}{2}
. As above, from diagram (
3.53
) and from
t>2
, we get
\mbox{deg}(M)>\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p)
so
there exists some effective divisor
E_{e,p}
of degree
e>0
for which
M=K_{C}-p-E_{e,p}
, because
\mathcal{F}_{u}
arises from a general element of a positive dimensional irreducible locus
\mathcal{U}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{2}
.
One gets (
3.54
) from which, with same reasoning as above, one deduces
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in W^{2}_{e+1}(C)
.

For
e+1=\frac{t}{2}<\frac{g+2}{2}
, as above, we get contradiction for
d\geq 3g-6\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\;\frac{g+3}{3}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
since, from Lemma
2.4
, one has
W^{2}_{e+1}(C)=\emptyset
. When otherwise
4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
and
e+1=\frac{t}{2}<\frac{g+2}{2}
, from Lemma
2.4

W^{2}_{e+1}(C)=\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}
so one has
2\nu\leq e+1=\frac{t}{2}
, which forces
t\geq 4\nu
and so
d=4g-4-t\leq 4g-4-4\nu
. Therefore, we get contradiction for

d\geq 4g-3-4\nu\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\;4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
.

If otherwise
e+1=\frac{t}{2}=\frac{g+2}{2}
, then
d=3g-6
which must be even, so also
g
has to be even. From the first part of the above arguments, one gets
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in W^{1}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)
which, from Lemma
2.3
, is such that
W^{1}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)=\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}\cup\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
. If we are in the first component, i.e.
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}
, then
|p+E_{e,p}|=g^{1}_{\nu}+B_{\frac{g+2}{2}-\nu}
and, with the same reasoning as above, one deduces that
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in W^{2}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)
. In such a case, when
d=3g-6\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\;\frac{g+3}{3}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
we get contradiction because, from Lemma
2.4
,
W^{2}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)=\emptyset
. When otherwise
4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
, once again one has
W^{2}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)=\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}
which forces
\frac{t}{2}=\frac{g+2}{2}\geq 2\nu
, i.e.
\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{4}
, so we get contradiction for

d=3g-6\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\frac{g+3}{4}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
. If otherwise
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subset W^{1}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)
, in this case one has
\rho(g,1,\frac{g+2}{2})=0
, i.e.
\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}})=0
whereas
g^{2}_{t}=g^{2}_{g+2}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subset W^{2}_{g+2}(C)
is general in the
g
-dimensional component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
, where the statement on the dimension follows from Lemma
2.4
. From (
3.54
) one has

3=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\leq h^{0}(g^{2}_{g+2}-(p+E_{e,p}))+h^{0}(p+E_{e,p}).

Since
h^{0}(g^{2}_{g+2})=3
, then
0\leq h^{0}(g^{2}_{g+2}-(p+E_{e,p}))\leq 2
because, as reminded above,
g^{2}_{g+2}
general is birationally very-ample whereas
p+E_{e,p}
is assumed to be in
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subset W^{1}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)
. Furthermore
h^{0}(g^{2}_{g+2}-(p+E_{e,p}))=2
cannot occur: otherwise either

g^{2}_{g+2}-(p+E_{e,p})\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
which has dimension

0
so, since also
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
whereas

\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}})=g
, we would get contradiction, or
otherwise
g^{2}_{g+2}-(p+E_{e,p})\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}
which, from Lemma
2.3
, is such that
\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}})=\frac{g+2}{2}-\nu
so once again, taking into account that
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}})
which is
0
-dimensional whereas

g^{2}_{g+2}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
varies in dimension
g
, we would get another contradiction. Notice that also
h^{0}(g^{2}_{g+2}-(p+E_{e,p}))=1
cannot occur, otherwise

g^{2}_{g+2}=(p+E_{e,p})+B_{\frac{g+2}{2}}=g^{1}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}+B_{\frac{g+2}{2}}
, where
B_{\frac{g+2}{2}}
an effective base-divisor, another contradiction. Thus the only possibility is
h^{0}(g^{2}_{g+2}-(p+E_{e,p}))=0
so, from the previous inequality, we deduce that
h^{0}(p+E_{e,p})\geq 3
, i.e.
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in W^{2}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)
. From Lemma
2.4
, we get contradiction for

d=3g-6\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\;\frac{g+3}{3}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
When otherwise
4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
, from Lemma
2.4

one has
W^{2}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)=\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}
, which forces

\frac{g+2}{2}-2\nu\geq 0
, i.e.
\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{4}
, therefore, once again, we get contradiction for
d=3g-6\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\frac{g+3}{4}\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
.

To sum-up (v) is completely proved.
∎

Proposition 3.42
.

Let
g\geq 9
,
\frac{g+3}{4}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
and
3g-6\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
be integers and let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
. Then,
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
admits a regular component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]
corresponds to a rank-
2
stable bundle
\mathcal{F}
of degree
d
and speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
, which fits into an exact sequence (
3.52
) as in Lemma
3.41
, where
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
general as in Lemma
2.4
, where necessarily
\frac{2}{3}g+2\leq t\leq g+2
and where
\mathcal{F}
satisfying all the properties
(i)
-
(v)
as in Lemma
3.41
.

Proof.

Notice that if
\mathcal{F}
as in the statement fits in (
3.52
) of Lemma
3.41
, then
d=\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{F})=4g-4-t
. Therefore, from bounds on
d
, one has
\frac{2}{3}g+2\leq t\leq g+2
as in the assumptions of Lemma
3.41
. Recall moreover that, from Lemma
3.41
, a general
\mathcal{F}_{u}
as in the assumptions therein turns out to be stable either for
d\geq 3g-6
, when
\frac{g+3}{3}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
, or for
d\geq 4g-3-4\nu
, when
4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
. Notice that, when
\nu\geq\frac{g+3}{4}
one has
4g-3-4\nu\leq 3g-6
; in particular, for any
\frac{g+3}{4}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
,
\mathcal{F}_{u}
as in Lemma
3.41
turns out to be stable for any
3g-6\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
which is the range of interest. This explains the assumptions on
\nu
in the above statement.

To construct the component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
, following Lemma
3.41
, we therefore need first to produce a positive dimensional irreducible locus
\mathcal{U}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{2}
in
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
with all properties listed therein. Consider
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
general where, from Lemma
2.4
,
\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}})=3t-2g-6\geq 0
, the latter inequality following from the bounds on
t
(arising from those on
d
).
Then, consider
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
which is of dimension
m:=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}))=h^{1}(-g^{2}_{t})=h^{0}(K_{C}+g^{2}_{t})=g-1+t\geq\frac{5}{3}g+1
, from the bounds on
t
. Using notation as in (
1.17
) and in Theorem
2.2
, one has
\ell:=h^{0}(K_{C})=g
and
r:=h^{1}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})=h^{0}(g^{2}_{t})=3
, by generality of
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
. Since
\ell\geq r
and
m\geq\ell+1
, from Theorem
2.2
,
v\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
general gives rise to a vector bundle
\mathcal{F}_{v}
of speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{v})=1
, since the corresponding coboundary map
\partial_{v}
is surjective, whereas the locus
\mathcal{W}_{1}\subsetneq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
as in (
2.2
) is not-empty, irreducible, of (
expected
) dimension
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{1})=m-c(1)=m-(\ell-r+1)=(g-1+t)-(g-3+1)=t+1
(recall (
2.3
). Moreover, for
w\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
general,
{\rm corank}(\partial_{w})=1
. Thus, considering
\mathcal{W}_{2}
defined as in (
2.2
), one has necessarily
\mathcal{W}_{2}\subsetneq\mathcal{W}_{1}
.

From (
2.3
), the expected codimension of

\mathcal{W}_{2}
in
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
, is given by
c(2)=2(\ell-r+2)=2(g-1)=2g-2
. In particular

{\rm expdim}(\mathcal{W}_{2})={\rm max}\{0,\;m-c(2)\}={\rm max}\{0,\;(g-1+t)-(2g-2)\}={\rm max}\{0,\;t-g+1\},

in other words
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is expected to be

\mathcal{W}_{2}=\mathcal{W}_{3}=\{\mathcal{F}_{0}=(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})\oplus K_{C}\}
, for any
\frac{2}{3}g+2\leq t\leq g-1
, and to be of dimension
j\in\{1,2,3\}
for
t=g+j-1
. The next result shows that this is not the case.

Claim 3.43
.

For any
\frac{2}{3}g+2\leq t\leq g+2
and for
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
general, the locus

\mathcal{W}_{2}\subsetneq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
is not-empty and it contains an irreducible
component
\mathcal{U}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{2}
which is uniruled, of
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{U}_{2})=2
and whose general point
u\in\mathcal{U}_{2}
gives rise to an exact sequence (
3.52
) as in Lemma
3.41
, i.e. with
{\rm corank}(\partial_{u})=2
. For
\frac{2}{3}g+2\leq t\leq g
, the component
\mathcal{U}_{2}
is superabundant, i.e.

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{U}_{2})>{\rm expdim}(\mathcal{W}_{2})={\rm max}\{0,\;t-g+1\}
.

Proof.

The superabundance of
\mathcal{U}_{2}
will follow from our previous computation
\text{expdim}(\mathcal{W}_{2})=\operatorname{max}\{0,t-g+1\}
and form the construction of
\mathcal{U}_{2}
.

To perform such construction observe that, since
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
is general, Lemma
2.4
ensures it is a complete, birationally very ample linear series. Thus, there exists an open dense
U\subseteq C
such that for any
p\in U
,
|g^{2}_{t}-p|
is a base-point-free pencil. This defines a
1
-dimensional irreducible locus in the Grassmannian
\mathbb{G}:=\mathbb{G}(2,H^{0}(g^{2}_{t}))
given by
\mathcal{V}_{2,U}:=\{[V_{2,p}:=H^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p)]\}_{p\in U}
. For each such
[V_{2,p}]\in\mathcal{V}_{2,U}
, we consider the restricted multiplication map
\mu_{V_{2,p}}:V_{2,p}\otimes H^{0}(K_{C})\to H^{0}(K_{C}+g^{2}_{t})
. By the base-point-free pencil trick

\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{V_{2,p}}))=h^{1}(g^{2}_{t}-p)=g+2-t.

Consequently,
\operatorname{dim}(\text{im}(\mu_{V_{2,p}}))=2g-(g+2-t)=g+t-2
.

Recall the definition of the degeneracy locus
\mathcal{W}_{2}\subset\text{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})\simeq H^{0}(K_{C}+g^{2}_{t})^{\vee}
:

\mathcal{W}_{2}=\{u\mid\exists V\subseteq H^{0}(g^{2}_{t}),\operatorname{dim}(V)\geq 2,\text{im}(\mu_{V})\subseteq\operatorname{ker}(u)\}.

Given
m:=\operatorname{dim}(H^{0}(K_{C}+g^{2}_{t}))=g+t-1
, the subspace
\text{im}(\mu_{V_{2,p}})
is a hyperplane in
H^{0}(K_{C}+g^{2}_{t})
. Therefore,
\mathcal{V}_{2,U}
induces an irreducible 1-dimensional locus
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}\subset\mathbb{P}(\text{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}))
. For a general
[u]\in\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
, we have
\text{coker}(\partial_{u})^{\vee}\simeq V_{2,p}
. The affine cone
\mathcal{U}_{2}
over
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
is thus a
2
-dimensional irreducible locus in
\mathcal{W}_{2}
satisfying the required properties.

We want to prove that
\mathcal{U}_{2}
is actually an irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
. Let
\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{2}
be any irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
containing
\mathcal{U}_{2}
. Since, for
u\in\mathcal{U}_{2}
general
{\rm corank}(\partial_{u})=2
, by semicontinuty on
\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}
and by the fact that
\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{2}
(so for
u^{*}\in\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}
general one has at least
{\rm corank}(\partial_{u^{*}})\geq 2
), one deduces that
u^{*}\in\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}
general has
{\rm corank}(\partial_{u^{*}})=2
. From the properties of the loci
\mathcal{U}_{2}
and
\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}
and from Lemma
3.41
,
u\in\mathcal{U}_{2}
and
u^{*}\in\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}
general give both rise to rank-two vector bundles
\mathcal{F}_{u}
and
\mathcal{F}_{u^{*}}
such that
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u^{*}})=3
and satisfying all the properties
(i)
-
(v)
therein. In particular, from Lemma
3.41
-
(i)
, both bundles
\mathcal{F}_{u}
and
\mathcal{F}_{u^{*}}
fit also in an exact sequence of the form
(u_{p})
, for
p\in C
general.

Since
h^{0}(K_{C}-p)=g-1
and
h^{1}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p)=h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p)=2
, the fact that
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u^{*}})=3
reads, in the vector space
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p)
, that
u,u^{*}
must be elements in

\left(\mathrm{coker}\left(H^{0}(K_{C}+g^{2}_{t}-2p)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Phi_{p}}}{{\leftarrow}}H^{0}(K_{C}-p)\otimes H^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p)\right)\right)^{\vee},

in other words they both have to belong to
\mathrm{im}(\Phi_{p})^{\perp}
, sub-vector space of
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p)
. Since,
|g^{2}_{t}-p|
is a base-point-free pencil, by the base-point-free pencil trick we have

\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\Phi_{p}))=h^{0}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})=h^{1}(g^{2}_{t})=g+2-t
as computed above. Thus,

\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{im}(\Phi_{p}))=2(g-1)-(g+2-t)=g+t-4
, whereas

h^{0}(K_{C}+g^{2}_{t}-2p)=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p))=g+t-3
from (
1.16
) since
K_{C}-p\ncong K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p
. Hence
\mathrm{im}(\Phi_{p})^{\perp}\simeq\mathbb{C}
is a
1
-dimensional sub-vector space in
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p)
. Since this holds for
p\in C
general and since, from Lemma
3.41
,
\mathcal{F}_{u^{*}}
admits all quotients
K_{C}-p
, for
p
varying in a suitable open dense subset
U\subseteq C
, this gives rise to a dominant map
\psi
defined as:

U\times\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}:\;\;\;(p,u_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{F}_{u_{p}},

where
u_{p}
is a vector in
\mathrm{im}(\Phi_{p})^{\perp}\simeq\mathbb{C}
and where
\mathcal{F}_{u_{p}}
is the corresponding bundle in
\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}
.

Thus
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*})=2
, so
\mathcal{U}_{2}=\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}
, i.e.
\mathcal{U}_{2}
is actually an irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
as desidered. The previous dominant map
\psi
shows also that
\mathcal{U}_{2}
is uniruled. This concludes the proof of the claim.
∎

From Lemma
3.41
-(v), for general
u\in\mathcal{U}_{2}
, the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is stable with speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=3
. To construct the component
\mathcal{B}_{\text{reg},3}\subseteq B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
, let
g^{2}_{t}
vary in an open dense subset
S\subseteq\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
. By Claim
3.43
,
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{U}_{2})\simeq U\subseteq C
since each
\mathbb{P}(\text{im}(\Phi_{p}^{\vee})^{\perp})
is a point. This defines an irreducible variety
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}^{\text{Tot}}
, birational to
S\times C
, with:

\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}^{\text{Tot}})=\operatorname{dim}(S)+1=(3t-2g-6)+1=3t-2g-5=\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}+1,

where the dimension of
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
follows from Lemma
2.4
and from
d=4g-4-t
. There is a therefore a natural rational modular map:

(3.55)

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}^{Tot}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\dashrightarrow}}&U^{s}_{C}(d)\\
u=(p,g^{2}_{t})&\longrightarrow&[\mathcal{F}_{u}],\end{array}

where
[u]=\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{im}(\Phi_{p})^{\perp})
and where

\mathrm{im}(\pi)\subseteq B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
.

Claim 3.44
.

The general fiber of
\pi
as in (
3.55
) is
1
-dimensional. In particular,
\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{im}(\pi))=\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}
.

Proof.

Let
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi)
be general, so
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
for
u=(g_{t}^{2},p)\in\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}^{Tot}
general.

Let
u^{\prime}=((g^{2}_{t})^{\prime},p^{\prime})\in\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{u}])
, so that
\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}\cong\mathcal{F}_{u}
. Since from (
3.52
) as in Lemma
3.41
one has
2K_{C}-(g^{2}_{t})^{\prime}=\det(\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}})\simeq\det(\mathcal{F}_{u})=2K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}
, then one must have
g^{2}_{t}=(g^{2}_{t})^{\prime}
. Therefore the fiber has to be contained in
U\subseteq C
, but from Lemma
3.41
-
(i)
, it has to consist of
U
and the claim is proved.
∎

To conclude that
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
is a dense subset in a regular component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
we need to compute the Petri map of the bundle arising from its general point.

Claim 3.45
.

For
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi)
general, the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
is injective. In particular the closure of
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
in
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
, denoted by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
, gives rise to an irreducible component which is regular.

Proof of Claim
3.45
.

The expected dimension
\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}
of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
follows from Claim
3.44
and from the density of
\text{im}(\pi)
. To establish that
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
is generically smooth, we show that the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
is injective for a general
[\mathcal{F}]\in\text{im}(\pi)
. Recall that such a bundle fits into the presentations:

(3.56)

(u):\;\;0\to K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}\to 0\quad\text{and}\quad(u_{p}):\;\;0\to K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-p\to 0,

where
p\in C
and
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
are general.

Let
\mathcal{E}_{u}:=\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}
. From
(u_{p})
, we obtain the extension
0\to\mathcal{O}_{C}(p)\to\mathcal{E}_{u}\to g^{2}_{t}-p\to 0
. For any
q\neq p
,
h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p-q)=1
due to the birational very ampleness of
g^{2}_{t}
(cf. Lemma
2.4
). Thus,
h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{u}(-q))\leq 1=h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{u})-2
, which implies that
\mathcal{E}_{u}
is globally generated.

Global generation of
\mathcal{E}_{u}
and
h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{u})=3
yield the evaluation sequence

0\to-g^{2}_{t}=\det(\mathcal{E}_{u})^{\vee}\to\mathcal{O}_{C}\otimes H^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{u})\to\mathcal{E}_{u}\to 0.

Thus, tensoring by
\mathcal{F}_{u}
and taking cohomology, one gets

(3.57)

0\longrightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t}))\longrightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\otimes H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})\,,

whence
\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}})\simeq H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t}))
.

Considering (
3.56
) and the exact sequence obtained by tensoring it with
-g^{2}_{t}
gives rise to the following exact diagram

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&0&&0&&0&&\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[3.44444pt]
0&\rightarrow&K_{C}-2g^{2}_{t}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t})&\rightarrow&K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}&\rightarrow&0\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[3.44444pt]
0&\rightarrow&K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}&\rightarrow&K_{C}&\rightarrow&0\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[3.44444pt]
0&\rightarrow&\mathcal{O}_{g^{2}_{t}}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}|_{g^{2}_{t}}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{O}_{g^{2}_{t}}&\rightarrow&0\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[3.44444pt]
&&0&&0&&0&&.\end{array}

First notice
h^{0}(K_{C}-2g^{2}_{t})=0
; indeed we consider the following commutative diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccc}H^{0}(g^{2}_{t})\otimes H^{0}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{g^{2}_{t}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(K_{C})&&\\[3.44444pt]
\uparrow{\boldsymbol{\iota}}&&\parallel&\\[3.44444pt]
V_{2}\otimes H^{0}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{V_{2}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(K_{C})&\end{array}

where
V_{2}
is a general two-dimensional linear sub-vector space of
H^{0}(g^{2}_{t})
and where
\boldsymbol{\iota}
is a natural injection. The map
\mu_{g^{2}_{t}}
is injective, since
g^{2}_{t}
is a general element of
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
, therefore by composition also

\mu_{V_{2}}
is injective. Since
\mathbb{P}(V_{2})=|V_{2}|
is a general sub-pencil of the
g^{2}_{t}
, then
|V_{2}|
is a base point free general pencil thus, the base-point-free pencil trick gives
h^{0}(K_{C}-2g^{2}_{t})=\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{V_{2}}))=0
, as stated.

Thus, passing to cohomology in the previous diagram, we get

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&&&0&&0&&\\[3.44444pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[3.44444pt]
&&0&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t}))&\rightarrow&H^{0}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})&\rightarrow&\cdots\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[3.44444pt]
0&\rightarrow&H^{0}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(K_{C})&\rightarrow&\cdots\\[3.44444pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[3.44444pt]
0&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{g^{2}_{t}})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{t}&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}|_{g^{2}_{t}})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{2t}&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{g^{2}_{t}})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{t}&\rightarrow&\cdots\end{array}

Assume by contradiction
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t}))\neq 0
; an element
0\neq x\in H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t}))
would map to a non-zero element in
H^{0}(K_{C})
through the top-right composition. However, the middle-column injection implies
x
is also in
H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})
for which
\alpha(x)=0\in H^{0}(K_{C})
. This contradicts the fact that the image of
H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t}))
in
H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})
must land in
\operatorname{ker}(\alpha)=H^{0}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
. Therefore,
H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t}))=0
, hence
\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}
is injective.
∎

The previous arguments conclude the proof of Proposition
3.42
.
∎
